{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{1}^{Q_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{2}^{Q_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{0}^{P_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{1}^{P_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{2}^{P_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{i}^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{i}^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{i}^{\ast}}

by the assumption that each
l_{n}:X_{n}\to X_{n+1}
is a quasi
F
-local extension. Therefore
\mathsf{colim}(F_{i}^{\ast})
is an equivalence. Note that we have the commuting square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{colim}(X_{n}^{Q_{i}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\infty}^{Q_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{colim}(X_{n}^{P_{i}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\infty}^{P_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{colim}(F_{i}^{\ast})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{i}^{\ast}}

in which the top and bottom maps are equivalences by the assumption that
P_{i}
and
Q_{i}
are compact. Therefore we conclude that
F_{i}^{\ast}:X_{\infty}^{Q_{i}}\to X_{\infty}^{P_{i}}
is an equivalence. It follows that
X_{\infty}
is
F
-local.
∎

Our goal is now to construct the initial quasi
F
-local extension of a type
X
, and use it to show that the subuniverse of
F
-local types is reflective. For any
i:I
, we have the maps

\displaystyle F_{i}:P_{i}\to Q_{i}

\displaystyle F_{i}^{\ast}:X^{Q_{i}}\to X^{P_{i}}.

In the following characterization of quasi
F
-local extensions we use the pushout-product
F_{i}^{\ast}\mathbin{\square}F_{i}
, which is defined as follows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{Q_{i}}\times P_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{Q_{i}}\times Q_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{P_{i}}\times P_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(X^{P_{i}}\times P_{i}\big)\sqcup^{\big(X^{Q_{i}}\times P_{i}\big)}\big(X^{Q_{i}}\times Q_{i}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{P_{i}}\times Q_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{i}^{\ast}\mathbin{\square}F_{i}}

by the universal property of pushouts. Also note that the outer square in the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{Q_{i}}\times P_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{Q_{i}}\times Q_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{P_{i}}\times P_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(X^{P_{i}}\times P_{i}\big)\sqcup^{\big(X^{Q_{i}}\times P_{i}\big)}\big(X^{Q_{i}}\times Q_{i}\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{ev}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{ev}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{i}}

commutes, so we get a map
\varepsilon_{i}
as indicated.

Lemma 2.5
.

For any type
l:X\to Y
, the type of
I
-indexed families of diagonal fillers for the squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{Q_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{Q_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{P_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{P_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l\circ\mathord{\hskip 1.0pt\text{--}\hskip 1.0pt}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathord{\hskip 1.0pt\text{--}\hskip 1.0pt}\circ F_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathord{\hskip 1.0pt\text{--}\hskip 1.0pt}\circ F_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l\circ\mathord{\hskip 1.0pt\text{--}\hskip 1.0pt}}

is equivalent to the type of morphisms
j:\Big(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(i:I)}}}{\sum_{(i:I)}}{\sum_{(i:I)}}{\sum_{(i:I)}}X^{P_{i}}\times Q_{i}\Big)\to Y
equipped with a homotopy witnessing that the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(i:I)}}}{\sum_{(i:I)}}{\sum_{(i:I)}}{\sum_{(i:I)}}\Big(\big(X^{P_{i}}\times P_{i}\big)\sqcup^{\big(X^{Q_{i}}\times P_{i}\big)}\big(X^{Q_{i}}\times Q_{i}\big)\Big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(i:I)}}}{\sum_{(i:I)}}{\sum_{(i:I)}}{\sum_{(i:I)}}X^{P_{i}}\times Q_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{tot}({\lambda}i.\,F_{i}^{\ast}\mathbin{\square}F_{i})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lambda}(i,t).\,\varepsilon_{i}(t)}

commutes.

Proof.

It suffices to show that for each
i:I
, the type of diagonal fillers in the first description is equivalent to the type of maps
j:X^{P_{i}}\times Q_{i}\to Y
equipped with a homotopy witnessing that the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Big(\big(X^{P_{i}}\times P_{i}\big)\sqcup^{\big(X^{Q_{i}}\times P_{i}\big)}\big(X^{Q_{i}}\times Q_{i}\big)\Big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{P_{i}}\times Q_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{i}^{\ast}\mathbin{\square}F_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{i}}

commutes. The type
X^{P_{i}}\to Y^{Q_{i}}
is equivalent to the type
X^{P_{i}}\times Q_{i}\to Y
by adjointness.

By the dependent universal property of pushouts established in
Section

1
, the type
l\circ\varepsilon_{i}\sim j\circ(F_{i}^{\ast}\mathbin{\square}F_{i})
is equivalent to the type of triples
(K,L,M)
consisting of

\displaystyle K

\displaystyle:{\rm Phys.\penalty\ Rev.\penalty\ D}{f:P_{i}\to X}{p:P_{i}}l(f(p))=j(f,F_{i}(p))

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle:{\rm Phys.\penalty\ Rev.\penalty\ D}{g:Q_{i}\to X}{q:Q_{i}}l(g(q))=j(g\circ F_{i},q)

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle:{\rm Phys.\penalty\ Rev.\penalty\ D}{g:Q_{i}\to X}{p:P_{i}}K(g\circ F_{i},p)=L(g,F_{i}(p)).

The type of such quadruples
(j,K,L,M)
is also equivalent to the fiber of the gap map

(X^{P_{i}}\to Y^{Q_{i}})\to\Big(X^{Q_{i}}\to Y^{Q_{i}}\Big)\times_{\big(X^{Q_{i}}\to Y^{P_{i}}\big)}\Big(X^{P_{i}}\to Y^{P_{i}}\Big)

at
(l^{Q_{i}},l^{P_{i}},\mathsf{htpy\underline{\ }{}refl})
.
∎

Definition 2.6
.

For any type
X
, we define the initial quasi
F
-local extension
l:X\to QL_{F}X
of
X
to be the pushout

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(i:I)}}}{\sum_{(i:I)}}{\sum_{(i:I)}}{\sum_{(i:I)}}\Big(\big(X^{P_{i}}\times P_{i}\big)\sqcup^{\big(X^{Q_{i}}\times P_{i}\big)}\big(X^{Q_{i}}\times Q_{i}\big)\Big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(i:I)}}}{\sum_{(i:I)}}{\sum_{(i:I)}}{\sum_{(i:I)}}X^{P_{i}}\times Q_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces QL_{F}X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{i}^{\ast}\mathbin{\square}F_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lambda}(i,t).\,\varepsilon_{i}(t)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l}

Remark 2.7
.

The initial quasi
F
-local extension of a type
X
is initial by the universal property of the pushout and the characterization of
Section

2
.

Example 2.8
.

Consider the family of maps consisting just of the terminal projection
\mathbf{2}\to\mathbf{1}
. We sketch an argument that the quasi
\mathbf{2}
-local extension of
X
is equivalent to
{X}\ast{X}
, i.e. that we have a pushout square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X^{2}\times\mathbf{2})\sqcup^{X\times\mathbf{2}}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X}\ast{X}}

To see this, we note first that the pushouts

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S+S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\sqcup^{S}B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A+B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A+B)\sqcup^{(S+S)}S}

are equivalent, for any span
A\leftarrow S\rightarrow B
. Therefore it follows that the pushouts

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times\mathbf{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{2}\sqcup^{X}X^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{2}\times\mathbf{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X^{2}\times\mathbf{2})\sqcup^{(X\times\mathbf{2})}X}

are equivalent. Therefore it suffices to compute the pushout

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{2}\sqcup^{X}X^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\sqcup^{(X^{2}\sqcup^{X}X^{2})}X^{2}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla_{\delta_{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\mathsf{htpy\underline{\ }{}refl}_{\mathsf{id}_{X}}]}

Now we observe that for any cube

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{111}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{110}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{101}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{011}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{100}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{010}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{001}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{000}}

the following are equivalent:

(i)

The cube is cocartesian.

(ii)

The the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{011}\sqcup^{A_{111}}A_{101}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{001}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{010}\sqcup^{A_{110}}A_{100}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{000}}

is cocartesian.

We cite
[
Munson
]
for this observation, and the analogous result in homotopy type theory is work in progress. Using the above equivalence, it follows that the pushout
X^{2}\sqcup^{X}X^{2}
is the colimit of the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}}

Furthermore, by the same equivalence we see that another way of computing this colimit is as the following pushout

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\sqcup^{X^{2}}X,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}

which is the join
{X}\ast{X}
.

Proposition 2.9
.

If
X
is
F
-local, then
l:X\to QL_{F}X
is an equivalence.

Proof.

If
X
is
F
-local, then each
F_{i}^{\ast}
is an equivalence. It follows that the pushout-product
F_{i}^{\ast}\mathbin{\square}F_{i}
is an equivalence. Since pushouts of equivalences are equivalences, the claim follows.
∎

Theorem 2.10
.

For any type
X
and any family
F:{\rm Phys.\penalty\ Rev.\penalty\ D}{a:A}P(a)\to Q(a)
of maps between
compact types, the subuniverse of
F
-local types is reflective.

Proof.

We define the localization
\eta:X\to LX
as the sequential colimit

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces QL_{F}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces QL_{F}^{2}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}

Since each
l:QL_{F}^{n}X\to QL_{F}^{n+1}X
has the structure of a quasi
F
-local extension, it follows by
Section

2
that
LX
is
F
-local. It remains to show that for any
F
-local type
Y
, the precomposition map

\eta^{\ast}:(LX\to Y)\to(X\to Y)

is an equivalence.

We first show that the map

l^{\ast}:(QL_{F}X\to Y)\to(X\to Y)

is an equivalence. To see this, note that we have a commuting triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{cocone}(Y)}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(QL_{F}X\to Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X\to Y)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{cocone\underline{\ }{}map}}

We note that by
Section

2
, the fiber of
\pi_{1}:\mathsf{cocone}(Y)\to(X\to Y)
is equivalent to the type of diagonal fillers of the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{Q_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{Q_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{P_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{P_{i}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l\circ\mathord{\hskip 1.0pt\text{--}\hskip 1.0pt}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathord{\hskip 1.0pt\text{--}\hskip 1.0pt}\circ F_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathord{\hskip 1.0pt\text{--}\hskip 1.0pt}\circ F_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l\circ\mathord{\hskip 1.0pt\text{--}\hskip 1.0pt}}

This type of diagonal fillers is contractible by
Section

2
, since
Y
is assumed to be
F
-local. In other words, the map
\pi_{1}:\mathsf{cocone}(Y)\to(X\to Y)
is an equivalence. Since
\mathsf{cocone\underline{\ }{}map}:(QL_{F}X\to Y)\to\mathsf{cocone}(Y)
is an equivalence by the universal property of
QL_{F}X
, we conclude by the 3-for-2 property that the map

l^{\ast}:(QL_{F}X\to Y)\to(X\to Y)

is an equivalence.

We conclude that any map
g:X\to Y
extends uniquely to a cocone on the type sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces QL_{F}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces QL_{F}^{2}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots,}

and therefore it follows that
\eta^{\ast}:(LX\to Y)\to(X\to Y)
is an equivalence.
∎

Corollary 2.11
.

For any family
N:I\to\mathcal{U}
of compact types, the subuniverse of
N
-null types is a modality.
